# Consideration of funding proposals - Addendum II

## Funding proposal package for FP125

### Summary

This addendum contains the following seven parts:

#### a) A funding proposal titled "Strengthening the resilience of smallholder agriculture to climate change induced water insecurity in the Central Highlands and South-Western Switzerland" (Proposal No. 17-17-2020).

#### b) One objective statement by the national designated authority(ies) or local point(s):

#### c) Environmental and social report(s) of disclosure:

#### d) Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment:

#### e) Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment.

#### f) Gender documentation.

<md> | A.2. Project Executive Summary (max 300 words) | 
 | --- | 
 | 1. Viet Nam is particularly vulnerable to climate change variability and change. Two of the regions most vulnerable to climate change are Cenral Highlands and South-Central Coast. Changes in rainfall patterns, increased frequency and intensity of droughts, declines in yields and incomes particularly harmful to rain fed lands and within this group, poor and near-poor farmers. | 
 | 2. The objective of this project is to empower vulnerable smallholder farmers for climate-resilient agriculture production by strengthening their access to information, knowledge and practices, and building the capacities of all farmers in Vietnam. This will be achieved through the following activities: | 
 | 3. The project was developed as part of an integrated programme funded through multiple sources, as envisaged by enhancing water security and building the climate change adaptation capacity of agroecosystem managers in Cenral Highlands and South-Central Coastal Regions. In this regard, the Government of Viet Nam (GoV) to adopt a paradigm shift in the way smallholder farmers are managing their farms through an integrated approach to agricultural resilience and adaptation to climate change will be used as a model for other provinces of the country. | 
 | 4. This project has been designed to achieve smallholder development through climate innovation and adaptation. The project aims to enhance the adaptive capacity of farmers, particularly women farmers, to manage climate risks and increase their incomes. It also aims to strengthen the institutional capacities for long-term multi-stakeholder coordination and management of climate change adaptation in Vietnam. | 
 | 5. This project will be implemented by the Government of Viet Nam (GoV), that is, through the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Fisheries (MAFF) and the Ministry of Rural Affairs (MRRA). The project will be implemented in five provinces of the Central Highlands and South-Central Coast regions of Vietnam. | 
 | 6. This project is funded through a USD 90 million loan from the Asian Development Bank, as well as USD 22.06 million from the Government of Vietnam. The funds will be used for the following purposes: (a) to achieve last mile connections in this particular focus on ethnic minority and women farmers; and (b) pass of climate-resilient agricultural practices, co-development of value chains and multi-stakeholder coordination for climate-resilient development. | 
 | 7. This project is part of an integrated approach to agricultural resilience that focuses on the following areas: (a) improving water security; and (b) building the climate change adaptation capacity of agroecosystem managers, with a focus on smallholder farmers. The project will also strengthen existing partnerships among value chain stakeholders to ensure addresses climate risks while also establishing or strengthening support to vulnerable smallholders. | 
 | 8. This project has been designed to achieve the following objectives: (a) to improve water security and build the climate change adaptation capacity of agroecosystem managers, with a focus on smallholder farmers; and (b) to strengthen existing partnerships among value chain stakeholders to ensure addresses climate risks while also establishing or strengthening support to vulnerable smallholders. The project will also strengthen existing partnerships among value chain stakeholders to ensure addresses climate risks while also establishing or strengthening support to vulnerable smallholders. |

<doc> Key sub-activities will include: 
 1.1.1 Install 185 km of new pressurized pipe systems, hydrants located at a reasonable distance from farmer 
 farmers to supply water for irrigation and drinking purposes. 
 1.1.2 Carry out modernization of main systems, installation of flow controls and measurement devices. 
 1.1.3 Provide new and improved weirs replacing farmer-constructed temporary weirs, permanent ponds/storages for 
 irrigating HVCs, and upgrades of upstream storage areas. 
 Activity 1.2: Establish last-mile connections between 
 farmer lands to help cope with increasing rainfall variability 
 84. This activity will support vulnerable poor/near-poor farmers by overcoming barriers to last-mile connection thus enhancing their access to sufficient, reliable and affordable water supply. In order to achieve this objective, the project will: 
 (a) provide technical assistance and training to farmer field managers in the design, construction and operation of WEIDAP irrigation infrastructure; (b) build 
 upon existing infrastructure by upgrading existing systems and constructing new ones; (c) support the development of a water management system for the 
 purpose of monitoring and controlling water use and distribution; (d) provide technical assistance to farmers in the design, construction and operation of 
 small-scale irrigation systems (e.g., drip irrigation, micro-spraying, etc.); (e) support the development of a water management system for the purpose of monitoring and controlling water use and distribution; <md> | Detailed Project Description | 
 | --- | 
 | 15. Governance Arrangements. The project will be governed by a Project Steering Committee (PSC) and the five DARDs (as Responsible Parties) as well as the five Provinces. The PSC is responsible for the overall management of the project, including its planning, implementation, monitoring, evaluation, and reporting. The five DARDs will be responsible for the technical aspects of the project, including its design, construction, operation, maintenance, and decommissioning. The five Provinces will be responsible for the social aspects of the project, including its impact on local communities, economy, and environment. | 
 | 161. The PSC will be comprised of: - An Executive (role represented by National Implementer (NIP) with the DARDs in collaboration with PPCs) of the five DARDs - A Project Manager (PM) representing the project owner, who is responsible for the overall management of the project and its implementation. The PM will be appointed from among the five DARDs or the five Provinces. - Senior Beneficiary representatives from five DARDs in Bin Thuan province as the Responsible Parties who will be involved in the decision-making process regarding the technical feasibility of the project, compliance with donor requirements and rules pertaining to use capacity as GCF AE. | 
 | 161. The PSC will be comprised of: - An Executive (role represented by National Implementer (NIP) with the DARDs in collaboration with PPCs) of the five DARDs - A Project Manager (PM) representing the project owner, who is responsible for the overall management of the project and its implementation. The PM will be appointed from among the five DARDs or the five Provinces. - Senior Beneficiary representatives from five DARDs in Bin Thuan province as the Responsible Parties who will be involved in the decision-making process regarding the technical feasibility of the project, compliance with donor requirements and rules pertaining to use capacity as GCF AE.

 Vulnerability and financing needs of the beneficiary country 
 E2377 Climate change is resulting in increasing deficits in 
 agricultural production with consequently increased intensity of poverty, 
 particularly for small-scale farmers vulnerable to reduced water 
 availability on rain fed lands, within this group, poor and near- 
 farmers, are particularly at risk. Yield reductions in crops, 
 livestock and national level revenues, incomes and employment, 
 affecting the livelihood of smallholder farmers, will significantly increase their vulnerability to 
 climate change impacts. In addition, the occurrence of extreme droughts, 
 especially in the Central Highlands and South-Central Coast regions. For both 
 regions, climate change is expected to severely impair 
 agricultural productivity as well as high rates of poverty. For the South-Central Coast,

<doc> F.3. Environmental, Social Assessment, including 
 and environmental risks that could arise from project 
 implementation and Social Management Framework. 
 Section G below, together with appropriate mitigation 
 measures. 
 321. The envisaged project has two inter-related Outputs: 
 • Output 1 : Enhanced water security for agricultural 
 of climate-induced rainfall variability and drought; 
 access to climate information, finance, and markets. 
 • Output 2 : Increased resilience of smallholder 
 farmers through climate-resilient agriculture and 
 improved adaptation to less demands on the natural environment. 
 322. Through delivery of the outputs, communities in Cambodia 
 will experience less damage from drought, improved adaptation to 
 less demands on the natural environment. 
 323. Benefits will include increased food security, improved 
 resources, more efficient irrigation and farming practices, 
 reduced vulnerability to climate-induced impacts associated with 
 production of maize and soybeans. Improved access to markets 
 and storage, grass-lined bunds, galleries, quincy plugs, etc. 
 324. Environmental impacts will be assessed in relation to the project's 
 interventions and their potential for long-term benefits to workers and 
 communities. 
 325. Key considerations in minimising environmental and 
 social impacts will include agroforestry, multi-cropping, cover crops, 
 impact and runoff velocities on the soil surface. One 
 will be slowed through adoption of practices such as 
 and storage, grass-lined bunds, galleries, quincy plugs, etc. 
 326. Controlled runoff and increased groundcover will reduce 
 erosion, both wind and water, which will benefit waterways 
 and improve soil biodiversity. 
 327. Environmental benefits will also accrue from diversified cropping systems also produce ancillary 
 populations, including pollinators. 
 328. Environmental impacts associated with the project are outlined in the ESMF, 
 and erosion control, and health and safety for workers and 
 the project to significant land degradation as a result of erosion 
 led by climate change. Reducing direct impact of rainfall on 
 crops will be achieved through the use of mulch, cover crops, crop residues of farm fields that helps to reduce raindrop 
 runoff and soil erosion, both wind and water, which will benefit waterways 
 consequence of adopting more climate-resilient agricultural 
 practices. The project will also contribute to the reduction of 
 increased diversity and abundance of insect 
 pests, thereby reducing pesticide use.

 352. Effective project implementation faces technical, operational, financial, social and environmental risks (please 
 see section F.3 for ESS assessment). Manageable risk management is crucial to ensure successful 
 irrigation systems. Construction of irrigation ponds and operation of water efficient irrigation technologies will take 
 considerable time and resources. The project may be subject to changes in the climate, which could affect the 
 performance of the project implementation. Inadequate vector control measures may result in fins to participate 
 and stop the infrastructure project. Major experience communication difficulties in Vietnam 
 limitations may obstruct timely termination of infrastructure project.

     among 
     farmers through Farmer

     climate risk mitigation measures, and learn 
     methods and practices of managing soil, water 
     and crop management resources to ensure ongoing 
     and adaptation to the impacts of climate change. Post FFS, these farmers will duplicate this 
     approach across the ten regions by returning to 
     farmers with support from government and 
     NGOs. 
     Under this project, farmers will be trained in 
     agroecosystem vulnerability to evolving climate 
     conditions and how to adapt their crops and livestock 
     practices. The ability to 
     analyse vulnerability to climate change will 
     help farmers to develop a policy approach that 
     supports the development of climate-resilient 
     projects, including risk management guidelines and enhances 
     the capacity of farmer champions from 
     other factors and conditions. This ability to 
     analyse vulnerability to climate change will 
     support the development of policies and programs 
     that are more effective in reducing the risks 
     associated with climate change. The project will also 
     build the capacities of farmer champions from 
     different sectors (e.g., agriculture, forestry, 
     livestock, fisheries) to develop and implement 
     measures and methodologies and tools that are 
     appropriate for their local context. 
 2.1 Technical assistance through Farmer Field Schools 
 In order to analyse their agroecosystem vulnerability 
 using traditional and knowledge and scientific 
 methods and practices of managing soil, water 
 and crop resources in an ongoing 
 manner, the project will provide technical assistance 
 to farmers through Farmer Field Schools. 
 The project will support the development of a 
 training programme for 200 participants in each of the 60 
 farmer champions in Years 1, 2 and 3. The training 
 programme will be conducted over two days (8 hours) 
 per participant, with a total of 40 participants 
 from over 21,200 farmers. The training programme 
 will cover the following topics: 
 • Establishing access to markets and credits 
 for sustainable investments by smallholder farmers and 
 value chain actors 
 • Identifying opportunities for climate-resilient 
 agricultural production, including training in acronyms for climate- 
 resilient crops, and the need to plan and implement 
 renewal required to sustain climate resiliency of 
 smallholder farmers and learn how to manage finance 
 and investment. The project will provide expert advice to lenders 
 and poor farmers for climate resilience of their agroecosystems, and market crops 
 for the development of the necessary financial products 
 and mechanisms. Lending institution staff will be

3. **Format for Markdown**: Once you have the text, format it according to Markdown guidelines. This includes using headings (#), bullet points (•), and other formatting elements to make the text more readable.

4. **Review and Edit**: Review the transcribed text for accuracy and clarity. Make any necessary edits to ensure that the content is clear and easy to understand.

5. **Save as Markdown**: Save the formatted text in a Markdown file, which can be opened in various text editors or IDEs.

Remember, the quality of the transcription will depend on the clarity and legibility of the original image. If the image has poor resolution or low contrast, it may be difficult to accurately transcribe the text.

# A Detailed Analysis of the Sustainability of the Project Interventions After Project Completion

## Introduction

This report provides a detailed analysis of the sustainability of the project interventions after project completion. The assessment is based on the assumption that the willingness to pay (WTP) for maintaining and operating the water assets by the beneficiaries remains constant over time.

## Methodology

The analysis was conducted using the following methodology:

1. **Data Collection**: Data on the WTP of the beneficiaries, the cost of maintaining and operating the water assets, and the financial contributions from the project interventions were collected.
2. **Model Development**: A model was developed to estimate the sustainability of the project interventions based on the WTP and the financial contributions.
3. **Sensitivity Analysis**: The sensitivity analysis was conducted to assess the impact of changes in the WTP and financial contributions on the sustainability of the project interventions.
4. **Conclusion**: Based on the results of the analysis, a conclusion was drawn regarding the sustainability of the project interventions after project completion.

## Results

The analysis revealed that the sustainability of the project interventions is dependent on the WTP and financial contributions of the beneficiaries. The sensitivity analysis showed that changes in the WTP and financial contributions can have a significant impact on the sustainability of the project interventions.

## Discussion

The results of the analysis suggest that the sustainability of the project interventions is dependent on the WTP and financial contributions of the beneficiaries. The sensitivity analysis showed that changes in the WTP and financial contributions can have a significant impact on the sustainability of the project interventions.

## Conclusion

The analysis revealed that the sustainability of the project interventions is dependent on the WTP and financial contributions of the beneficiaries. The sensitivity analysis showed that changes in the WTP and financial contributions can have a significant impact on the sustainability of the project interventions.

## Recommendations

Based on the results of the analysis, the following recommendations are made:

1. **Increase the WTP**: To ensure the sustainability of the project interventions, it is recommended to increase the WTP of the beneficiaries by providing them with incentives and benefits for maintaining and operating the water assets.
2. **Increase Financial Contributions**: To ensure the sustainability of the project interventions, it is recommended to increase financial contributions from the project interventions by providing them with incentives and benefits for maintaining and operating the water assets.
3. **Monitor and Evaluate**: It is recommended to monitor and evaluate the sustainability of the project interventions regularly to ensure that they are meeting the needs of the beneficiaries and achieving their objectives.

## Conclusion

The analysis revealed that the sustainability of the project interventions is dependent on the WTP and financial contributions of the beneficiaries. The sensitivity analysis showed that changes in the WTP and financial contributions can have a significant impact on the sustainability of the project interventions. Based on the results of the analysis, it is recommended to increase the WTP and financial contributions from the project interventions to ensure their sustainability.

<doc> Recommendation 2: Assign WWU staff members to provide handholding support to ethnic 
 minorities who wish to participate in consultations and other group activities. 
 Recommendation 3: Tailor materials and approaches to meet the unique needs of women and 
 ethnic minorities. 
 Recommendation 4: Ensure that gender equality is a key priority for all actions taken by WWU. 
 Recommendation 5: Provide training on gender equality in the existing institutional, policy and legal 
 framework. The focus should be on the promotion of gender equality in the workplace, in the 
 management of resources and in the development of policies and programs. 
 Recommendation 6: Ensure that women are adequately represented in all decision-making processes at WWU. 
 Recommendation 7: Provide training to staff on how to identify and address issues related to gender 
 equality in their workplace. 
 Recommendation 8: Establish mechanisms for monitoring the progress of gender equality initiatives. 
 Recommendation 9: Ensure that women are adequately represented in all decision-making processes at WWU. 
 Recommendation 10: Provide training to staff on how to identify and address issues related to gender 
 equality in their workplace. </doc>
